# Habit: Daily routine tracker / 継続する技術 (App Store ID 1120239484) — Full Review Analysis

**bondavi Inc. · `info.todice.habit` · "Goal planner & Streaks"**

Every one of the **2,290 reviews** in `reviews.jsonl` was read individually, in its original language, and hand-coded. Nothing was sampled, keyword-filtered or skipped. Corpus: **2016-06-20 → 2026-09-06**, **11 storefronts**, **4 languages** (Japanese 2,264, Chinese 18, English 7, Russian 1), corpus mean **4.73★**.

---

## How to read this

- Every quantified claim carries **count, percentage, denominator, scope, period, signal band and review IDs**. Signal bands are those in `Store Review Analysis Prompt.md`.
- Theme counts are **non-exclusive**: a review carrying five ideas is counted in five themes. Percentages are *shares of reviews mentioning a thing* and sum past 100%.
- **§10 is a complete per-review index.** Any number here resolves to exact reviews; any review resolves to the findings it supports via §9.J.
- Review IDs appear as `` `1712808912` `` (the `review_id` in `reviews.jsonl`) and as `#106` (position in date order, 1…2,290). Both appear in §10.
- Quotations are verbatim and short. Non-English text is quoted in the original with a translation. `…` marks an elision.
- Global percentages use **all 2,290 reviews**. Because 98.65% of them are Japanese, "global" and "Japan" are nearly the same population (§6).

### ⚠️ Eight warnings before any number here is used

1. **This is a Japanese corpus.** jp holds 2,259 of 2,290 reviews (98.65%). No other storefront reaches 50 reviews; together the other 10 hold 31. Nothing here describes non-Japanese markets except as limited evidence (§6.6).
2. **It is overwhelmingly positive.** 5★ 1,948 (85.07%); 1★ and 2★ together 76 (3.32%). A 1% theme is about 23 reviews, so the negative findings are small in share but specific and repeated.
3. **The corpus agrees with the public rating.** Apple's lookup API (2026-09-14) gives the JP listing 4.78★ from 53,106 ratings; the corpus mean is 4.73★. Written reviews are about 4.3% of JP ratings.
4. **Reviewers are mostly people for whom it worked.** Many reviews are written to mark a milestone (`REV_MILESTONE`), and the app has an in-app "rate this app" menu item (§9.H). People who quietly gave up rarely return to write; the few who did are in `FAIL_ADMIT` (§3.7).
5. **Outcomes are self-reported.** Streaks, weight loss and exam passes are the reviewer's account. One review claims 10 kg in a week (#2250) and is coded as stated.
6. **Purchase evidence is thin and pricing comes from reviewers.** `MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` covers 54 reviews (2.36%). The App Store page could not be retrieved, so add-on names and prices are what reviewers wrote (§9.G). No revenue, conversion or retention figure is derivable.
7. **Volume peaked in 2018 and has fallen.** E2 (2018-03-14 → 2019-12-31) holds 844 reviews; E5 (2025 →) holds 211. Late-period shares rest on a few hundred reviews.
8. **Codes are hand judgement.** Each line in the classification file quotes the text behind its codes (§1.4); nothing was assigned by keyword or model.

# PART 0 — READ THIS FIRST

The seven findings that should change a decision.

## 0.1 The product works by subtraction: one small goal, one tap, thirty days

`PR_SIMPLE` **448** reviews, **19.56%** of 2,290 — High-priority signal, mean 4.90★, 2016-06-20 → 2026-09-06. `PR_CONCEPT` **262** reviews, **11.44%** of 2,290; `PR_ONE_GOAL` **148** reviews, **6.46%** of 2,290. The limit reviewers first resent is the one they end up defending: `ONE_GOAL_CONVERT` **28** reviews, **1.22%** of 2,290 describe disliking the one-goal rule at first and agreeing with it later.

The outcome evidence is broad: `U_OUTCOME` **992** reviews, **43.32%** of 2,290 — High-priority signal, mean 4.92★, 2016-07-17 → 2026-09-06. It splits into streaks (`OUT_STREAK` **617** reviews, **26.94%** of 2,290), chronic quitters who say they finally continued (`OUT_QUITTER` **299** reviews, **13.06%** of 2,290), behaviour that became automatic (`OUT_HABIT` **194** reviews, **8.47%** of 2,290) and concrete life results (`OUT_RESULT` **157** reviews, **6.86%** of 2,290).

> "最初は制約が多くて使いにくいアプリだなと思いましたが、それは制作者さんの続けるための哲学にしっかりと基づいたものなのだと" — #36, `1558166349`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: At first I thought it was hard to use, with too many restrictions, but I realised they are firmly based on the maker's philosophy of how to keep going)*

> "アプリの方から「本当にそれ5分でできる？」と訊いてくれたのがとてもありがたかった" — #2187, `13314939713`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: I was really grateful that the app itself asked me, "can you really do that in 5 minutes?")*

> "信じてください。続きます。" — #1349, `5953466116`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: Believe me. It sticks.)*

The last is the most-voted review in the corpus (354 votes). The setup flow that pushes goals down to five minutes or less is itself praised (#1941, #2055, #2252). It is a product feature, not just copy.

**Evidence:** `1439846457`, `2003749013`, `2514046620`, `2778560514`, `3528637702`, `4631410244`, `5586358701`, `6890543813`, `8506800775`, `11538765911`.

## 0.2 The encouragement layer keeps people coming back, and its voice now drives the harshest reviews

`PR_COMMENTS` **428** reviews, **18.69%** of 2,290 — High-priority signal, mean 4.93★, 2017-02-21 → 2026-09-06. `PR_NOTIF` **203** reviews, **8.86%** of 2,290; `PR_HUMOR` **106** reviews, **4.63%** of 2,290; `PR_TIPS` **102** reviews, **4.45%** of 2,290. The daily one-line comment from the stick figure, and the reminder that says「やったれ！」("go for it!"), are named more often than any feature except simplicity.

> "褒められることに飢えていた俺にとってこの機能は本当に最高。" — #1197, `5316192055`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: For someone starved of praise like me, this feature is truly the best.)*

> "私の怠け心を見透かしたかのようなタイミングで2度目の通知が届きました。" — #1785, `8737413152`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: The second notification arrived at a moment as if it had seen through my laziness.)*

The same voice alienates a minority, and loudly. Tone and notification complaints (`NEG_COMMENT_TONE` ∪ `NEG_NOTIF_ANNOY`) are 14 reviews (0.61% of 2,290), mean 2.50★, and 9 of them fall in E4 (2022-05 → 2024). They include notifications that continue after being switched off:

> "これじゃ893と変わらないんですけど。" — #1627, `7553106036`, jp, 1★
> *(translation: This is no different from the yakuza.)*

> "通知をオフにしたはずなのに、通知がやたらくる。" — #1954, `10598709745`, jp, 1★
> *(translation: I definitely turned notifications off, yet they keep coming.)*

> "ノリが寒すぎる…。" — #1950, `10527702382`, jp, 1★
> *(translation: The jokey tone is way too cringey.)*

#1627 carries 18 votes. One long-term user asks for exactly the control that is missing: "応援コメントや診断？を完全オフにできるようにしてほしい" *(please let me switch the encouragement comments fully off)* (#2029, `11568521715`, jp, 4★).

**Evidence:** `1549417557`, `2291056093`, `3517716465`, `5530221276`, `7511954823`, `9792912850`; `2285682176`, `3959571379`, `10058846156`, `10317633924`, `10527702382`, `10548585183`, `10830806624`, `12446202458`.

## 0.3 The top feature request collides with the core design

`REQ_MULTI` **44** reviews, **1.92%** of 2,290 — Meaningful signal, mean 4.39★, 2016-06-24 → 2026-09-06, against `PR_ONE_GOAL` **148** reviews, **6.46%** of 2,290 that praise the limit. The request is growing: 4.7% of E5 reviews ask for it, the highest share of any era (§7.2).

The request is narrower than "let me add many habits". Reviewers want to **keep a habit they have already completed** while starting the next one. Today the finished habit stops being reminded and fades:

> "30日を達成して、新しい習慣を始めると前の習慣は通知されなくなります。" — #2131, `12651030354`, jp, 3★
> *(translation: Once you complete 30 days and start a new habit, the old habit is no longer notified.)*

> "並行して3つくらいの目標が記録出来るようにして欲しい。" — #2290, `14519088452`, jp, 3★
> *(translation: Please let me record about three goals in parallel.)*

> "I wish if I can add just one more task, it would be so good 2 tasks a day." — #2081, `12157880557`, au, 5★

Several reviewers volunteer the compromise themselves: unlock a second goal only after a first 30-day cycle is completed (#954, #1789, #2094).

**Evidence:** `1399663066`, `1949543359`, `2634012321`, `3747568189`, `4781242040`, `5491215598`, `6602410611`, `8886936066`, `12296276654`, `13760782567`.

## 0.4 The rules are strict, and real life breaks them

`U_RULE_FRICTION` **114** reviews, **4.98%** of 2,290 — Very strong signal, mean 4.06★, 2016-06-24 → 2026-09-06. The two-day reset, the fixed 30-day cycle and the day boundary are the product's discipline. Reviewers praise that discipline (`PR_RESET_TENSION` **40** reviews, **1.75%** of 2,290) and are hurt by it when illness, a menstrual period or a school trip interrupts them (`REQ_PAUSE` **10** reviews, **0.44%** of 2,290; `NEG_RESET_RULE` **14** reviews, **0.61%** of 2,290).

> "体調悪いとできないこともあると思う" — #106, `1712808912`, jp, 1★
> *(translation: sometimes you can't do it when you're unwell)*

> "生理が来ると、2日くらいお休みしたい人も中にはいるのです。" — #2027, `11522188751`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: When our period comes, some of us want about two days off.)*

The reset has split users since 2018. When an update in March 2018 removed the automatic reset, `PR_RESET_REMOVED` **6** reviews, **0.26%** of 2,290 welcomed it and `REQ_RESET_BACK` **13** reviews, **0.57%** of 2,290 asked for it back, the latter as late as 2024:

> "今回のアップデートでリセット機能が廃止され私的には嬉しいです" — #358, `2304355241`, jp, 4★
> *(translation: I'm personally glad this update abolished the reset.)*

> "リセット無いとモチベーションが格段に落ちる人もいるのです。" — #360, `2306392656`, jp, 4★
> *(translation: For some people motivation drops sharply without the reset.)*

Strength-training users add a structural objection: rest days are part of the training, so a daily rule does not fit (`REQ_FREQ` **8** reviews, **0.35%** of 2,290; #1917, #2239).

**Evidence:** `1399663066`, `1663994021`, `2316192213`, `2676574027`, `4438576334`, `5696425738`, `7086490482`, `9058462193`, `11353179556`, `12884112714`.

## 0.5 Defects hit the one action that matters: recording today's success

`U_BUG_ANY` **104** reviews, **4.54%** of 2,290 — Very strong signal, mean 3.29★, 2017-02-27 → 2026-08-14. That is a small share, but defects are the most common reason for a one-star review: 24 of 50 1★ reviews (48.00%) carry a defect code. The defects cluster on recording and reminding:

| Code | Meaning | n | % of 2,290 | Band | Mean ★ |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `BUG_NOTIF` | Wrong / unwanted / missing notifications | 38 | 1.66% | Meaningful signal | 3.42 |
| `BUG_INPUT` | Cannot record a day | 20 | 0.87% | Emerging signal | 2.75 |
| `BUG_COUNT` | Day count wrong | 12 | 0.52% | Emerging signal | 2.83 |
| `BUG_RESET` | Unexpected reset | 7 | 0.31% | Weak signal | 3.43 |
| `BUG_DISPLAY` | Wrong label / display glitch | 13 | 0.57% | Emerging signal | 4.15 |
| `BUG_CRASH` | Crashes | 5 | 0.22% | Weak signal | 1.80 |
| `BUG_IAP` | In-app purchase stuck / not restored | 4 | 0.17% | Weak signal | 3.25 |
| `BUG_KEYBOARD` | Keyboard hides button in setup | 3 | 0.13% | Weak signal | 3.33 |

> "キャンセルは右上のボタンからというポップアップが出てしまい、仕方なく消すとまた初日からやり直しです。" — #1314, `5861217675`, jp, 1★
> *(translation: A pop-up saying "cancel with the top-right button" appears; when I reluctantly delete it I'm back to day one.)*

> "私の5分間の頑張りが記録できず無駄になりました" — #1565, `7136283910`, jp, 1★
> *(translation: my five minutes of effort couldn't be recorded and went to waste)*

> "中確率程度で「前日の記録が帳消しになる」という深刻なバグが存在する。" — #2227, `13935906932`, jp, 3★
> *(translation: There is a serious bug that, fairly often, wipes out the previous day's record.)*

The "cancel" loop (`BUG_INPUT`) is reported from 2017 to 2026. For a product whose whole promise is an unbroken record, a lost day is the worst possible failure.

**Evidence:** `1553783592`, `1960898756`, `2104964546`, `2577618701`, `3719986876`, `4677332909`, `5998212733`, `6614785939`, `7566282416`, `10172688755`.

## 0.6 Money comes from patronage, which works for a minority and is priced for fewer

The JP listing (external, §9.E) states the app is 「完全無料」 and that in-app purchase is "only for people who want to support" it. The corpus confirms the model: `MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` **54** reviews, **2.36%** of 2,290 say they bought the add-on, `MON_WILL_PAY` **90** reviews, **3.93%** of 2,290 say they intend to, and `MON_SUPPORT_DEV` **39** reviews, **1.70%** of 2,290 frame paying as supporting the developer rather than unlocking a function.

> "自分も男気をみせて課金したりました" — #155, `1834959691`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: so I showed some spirit too and paid)*

> "無料で使わせていただいていることに罪悪感を感じたこと" — #1725, `8317723251`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: I felt guilty about using it for free)*

Price is the barrier: `MON_PRICE_HIGH` **20** reviews, **0.87%** of 2,290 and `MON_NO_PAY` **17** reviews, **0.74%** of 2,290 (students, minors and tight budgets). The add-on cost ¥960–980 as a one-off. Since May 2022 reviewers describe it as monthly (`MON_SUBSCRIPTION` **7** reviews, **0.31%** of 2,290; first 2022-05-15). Several name the price they would pay:

> "いくらなんでも980円は高すぎますね。300円くらいなら全然出すけど。" — #1262, `5601520030`, jp, 3★
> *(translation: ¥980 is far too much. I'd happily pay around ¥300.)*

> "月額980円(2023年4月時点)なので、ちょっと高いかな〜と思います。" — #1903, `9872904162`, jp, 3★
> *(translation: It's ¥980 a month as of April 2023, which feels a bit expensive.)*

> "１回100で何度でも寄付できる機能を付けてください。" — #2177, `13157065902`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: Please add a way to donate ¥100 at a time, as often as I like.)*

Post-purchase and purchase-prompt complaints are few (11 reviews, mean 2.18★) and concern the add-on's content, intrusive prompts and undelivered purchases:

> "課金後と従来のコメントの違いがわからないもの。" — #1577, `7262494547`, jp, 1★
> *(translation: I can't tell the difference between the comments before and after paying.)*

**Evidence:** `1725181542`, `2062336135`, `2633107549`, `3660959512`, `5410844385`, `6171429740`, `7262494547`, `8317723251`; `1934451692`, `2121147199`, `2587967121`, `5164444747`, `6480344552`, `8216943924`, `8730129908`, `10068902732`.

## 0.7 The developer's voice is a differentiator that is fading from the reviews

`U_DEV_BOND` **364** reviews, **15.90%** of 2,290 — High-priority signal, mean 4.92★, 2017-02-14 → 2026-09-04. Reviewers praise the developer's attitude, essays and release notes (`PR_DEV` **311** reviews, **13.58%** of 2,290; `PR_RELNOTES` **28** reviews, **1.22%** of 2,290), and install the developer's other apps (`XSELL_OTHER_APP` **78** reviews, **3.41%** of 2,290). This goodwill is what the purchase model runs on (§5.2).

> "レビューへの返信も一つずつなさっているアプリも初めて見ました。" — #130, `1767172314`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: It's also the first app I've seen that replies to every review one by one.)*

The share is falling: `PR_DEV` is 18.5% of E2 reviews and 5.2% of E5. Unanswered support mail is reported in `SUP_NONE` **5** reviews, **0.22%** of 2,290, about purchases, a notification defect or a feature request:

> "メールを送りました。が、返信がありませんでした。" — #1539, `6986884299`, jp, 3★
> *(translation: I sent an email. But there was no reply.)*

**Evidence:** `1544232487`, `2246132063`, `2693128722`, `3605386774`, `4385841788`, `5290093633`, `6130817348`, `8072590521`; `6173882157`, `6572812402`, `6986884299`, `7262494547`, `7761884378`.

# PART 1 — SCOPE AND METHOD

## 1.1 Files used

| File | Role | Records |
|---|---|---:|
| `App Store Reviews/70. Habit - Daily routine tracker - Goal planner & Streaks/reviews.jsonl` | **the corpus**; every claim resolves here | 2,290 |
| `by_country/*.jsonl` | 11 per-storefront files | 2,290 (union) |
| `manifest.json` | extraction metadata, per-country counts, rating distribution | — |
| `_state.json` | collection state per polled storefront | — |
| Apple iTunes Lookup API (external, 2026-09-14) | public rating, listing text, version, languages | 4 storefronts |

## 1.2 Schema actually present

All 13 fields are present on all 2,290 records; no title or body is empty.

| Field | Used for | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `review_id` | primary key | 2,290 distinct; zero duplicates |
| `country`, `country_name` | §6 | 11 storefronts |
| `rating` | §4 | integers 1–5 only |
| `title`, `body` | **read in full for every record** | median body length 64 characters |
| `author` | authenticity tests only (§9.H) | zero repeated names |
| `date` | §7 | 2016-06-20 → 2026-09-06 |
| `vote_count`, `vote_sum` | §9.H | 227 reviews have votes; max 354 |
| `is_edited` | §9.H | 11 edited |
| `app_id`, `app_name` | constant (`1120239484`, `Habit: Daily routine tracker`) | |

**Not present, and therefore never claimed:** app version per review, purchase records, developer responses, device, and any identifier linking a reviewer across storefronts.

## 1.3 Coverage and reconciliation

`70-reconcile.py` output, verbatim:

```
main records                 : 2290
distinct review_id           : 2290
duplicate review_id          : 0 []
exact content duplicates     : 0
repeat (storefront,author)   : 0
authors on >1 storefront     : 0
by_country files             : 11
sum of country file records  : 2290
country files == main subset : YES
storefronts in main          : 11
manifest total_reviews       : 2290  match=True
manifest storefronts         : 11  match=True
manifest per-country mismatch: 0 []
manifest rating dist match   : True  ({1: 50, 2: 26, 3: 64, 4: 202, 5: 1948})
corpus mean rating           : 4.7345  manifest 4.734  match=True
_state storefronts probed    : 38
_state all complete          : True
_state collected mismatch    : 0 []
_state zero-review fronts    : 27
sum _state collected         : 2290
field presence               : {'review_id': 2290, 'app_id': 2290, 'app_name': 2290, 'country': 2290, 'country_name': 2290, 'rating': 2290, 'title': 2290, 'body': 2290, 'author': 2290, 'date': 2290, 'vote_count': 2290, 'vote_sum': 2290, 'is_edited': 2290}
empty title / body           : 0 / 0
ratings outside 1-5          : 0
date range                   : 2016-06-20T11:13:48Z -> 2026-09-06T21:24:30Z
app_id values                : {'1120239484'}
app_name values              : {'Habit: Daily routine tracker'}
is_edited true               : 11
vote_count>0                 : 227, max 354
vote_sum negative            : 0
```

**Reviews read: 2,290/2,290 = 100%. Reviews hand-coded: 2,290/2,290 = 100%.** No deduplication was applied: there are no technical duplicates. 8 short generic bodies recur across different authors, months to years apart (§9.H); they are kept.

## 1.4 Processing method

1. `70-dump.py` emits all 2,290 reviews in date order with a stable index (1…2,290).
2. Reviews were read in **23 batches of up to 100**, in order, in full, in the original language. Translations quoted here were checked against the source string.
3. Each review received hand codes plus a note quoting the text that drove them (`70-cls/*.txt`, 2,290 lines).
4. **After every batch**, `70-check-cls.py` re-asserted each line's `review_id` at its index and, from batch 10 on, that every quoted fragment in the note occurs in that review. This caught drifted IDs in the early batches (§9.C).
5. `70-build-classification.py` rejects unknown codes, adds mechanical codes (`STAR_n`, `ERA_En`) and derives 9 unions.
6. `70-validate.py` runs the data checks in §9.C.
7. `70-build-report.py` computes every number and chooses every representative ID from the data at build time. It **refuses to write** if any quotation is not found verbatim in the review it is attributed to, and asserts every trend direction stated in the narrative.
8. `70-verify-report.py` re-reads the written file and checks it against the corpus (§9.M).

**Classification is hand judgement, not an algorithm.** No clustering, keyword rule or model assigned a code.

## 1.5 Signal thresholds

| Share of reviews | Label |
|---|---|
| < 0.1% | Ignore by default |
| 0.1% – < 0.5% | Weak signal |
| 0.5% – < 1% | Emerging signal |
| 1% – < 3% | Meaningful signal |
| 3% – 5% | Very strong signal |
| > 5% | High-priority signal |

At n=2,290, 0.1% ≈ 2 reviews, 1% ≈ 23 and 5% ≈ 115. Segment rates (within a star band, era or the paid segment) are labelled as segment rates and show both denominators; their band column is a segment band, not a corpus band.

## 1.6 Limitations, stated plainly

- **Selection.** Reviews over-represent people who succeeded and want to say thank you. The corpus measures what satisfied users value and what breaks for engaged users; it does not measure how many people quit silently.
- **One market.** Only Japan supports standalone analysis.
- **No version data.** Rule changes are dated from what reviewers say, not from a release history.
- **Pricing.** The in-app purchase list could not be retrieved; prices are reviewer statements with dates.
- **Thin late period.** E5 has 211 reviews; late trends are directional.

# PART 2 — PRODUCT AND MONETIZATION MODEL

## 2.1 What the product is, from the reviews

A single-habit tracker. The user sets one goal, guided toward something doable in five minutes or less, and picks the days and a reminder time. Each success is recorded with one tap on a ring that fills over a 30-day cycle. A stick-figure character shows a different line of encouragement after each tap. Missing two consecutive days prompts a reset; the previous day can be recorded until 3 a.m. Completed cycles go to a History screen. The app also carries short columns on habit formation and essays by the developer. An optional add-on (「無駄機能」, "useless feature") unlocks alternative message packs, including a 「中二病」 ("teenage-melodrama") voice.

| Capability | Positive evidence | Friction evidence |
|---|---|---|
| One goal at a time, guided small-goal setup | `PR_ONE_GOAL` 148, `PR_CONCEPT` 262, `ONE_GOAL_CONVERT` 28 | `REQ_MULTI` 44 |
| 30-day cycle | `OUT_STREAK` 617 | `NEG_PERIOD_FIXED` 5, `REQ_PERIOD` 13, `REQ_REPEAT_GOAL` 4, `NEG_90_REMOVED` 3 |
| One-tap record; previous day until 3 a.m. | `PR_SIMPLE` 448, `PR_GRACE` 41, `PR_FAST` 7 | `NEG_DAYBOUNDARY` 11, `NEG_PREVDAY_FLOW` 11, `BUG_INPUT` 20 |
| Two-day reset prompt | `PR_RESET_TENSION` 40 | `NEG_RESET_RULE` 14, `REQ_RESET_BACK` 13, `REQ_PAUSE` 10 |
| Encouragement line after each tap | `PR_COMMENTS` 428, `PR_HUMOR` 106 | `REQ_MORE_COMMENTS` 21, `NEG_COMMENT_TONE` 7 |
| Reminder notifications | `PR_NOTIF` 203 | `BUG_NOTIF` 38, `NEG_NOTIF_ANNOY` 9, `REQ_NOTIF_EDIT` 10 |
| History of cycles | `PR_HISTORY` 31 | `REQ_CALENDAR` 9, `REQ_HISTORY` 7, `REQ_MEMO` 7 |
| Columns and developer essays | `PR_TIPS` 102, `PR_RELNOTES` 28 | `NEG_RELNOTES_LONG` 1 |
| Passcode lock | `PR_PRIVACY` 4 | `REQ_LOCK` 2 |
| Paid message packs (無駄機能) | `MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` 54, `MON_MUDA_AWARE` 44 | `MON_PRICE_HIGH` 20, `MON_SUBSCRIPTION` 7, `BUG_IAP` 4, `MON_PAID_DISAPPOINT` 2 |
| Not present: widget, Apple Watch, iPad landscape, sync | — | `REQ_WIDGET` 17, `REQ_WATCH` 5, `REQ_IPAD` 13, `REQ_SYNC` 4 |

## 2.2 What the store listing says (external, Apple Lookup API, accessed 2026-09-14)

| Storefront | Listing title | Public mean ★ | Ratings |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| jp | 継続する技術｜ダイエットも筋トレも記録・習慣化で目標達成 | 4.78 | 53,106 |
| cn | 持续：习惯打卡与目标记录 | 4.88 | 99 |
| us | Habit: Goal & Streak Tracker | 4.92 | 60 |
| tw | 持續：習慣養成・生活習慣・目標管理 | 4.96 | 49 |

- Seller **bondavi Inc.**; Health & Fitness; age rating 4+; released 2016-06-18; current version **1.34.3**, dated **2026-09-10**; price 無料; 33 declared language codes.
- JP listing (verbatim): 「完全無料。（アプリ内課金は、応援したい人だけのものです。なくても全く困りません）」 *(Completely free. In-app purchase is only for people who want to support us; you lose nothing without it.)* and 「広告なし。（そのため、清々しいほど儲かりません）」 *(No ads. That is why we make refreshingly little money.)*
- JP listing claims 「毎月12,000人が「30日継続」」 *(12,000 people a month complete 30 days)* and describes input as 「1日3秒で入力」 *(three seconds a day)*. These are the developer's claims and are not used as evidence.
- US listing: "the app only allows you to set one goal at a time."
- The current JP release note is a serialised essay about writing a novel, not a change log. Reviewers treat these notes as content (`PR_RELNOTES`).

## 2.3 Free / paid / trial / unclear — classified

| Capability or offer | Classification | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Core tracker: goal, reminders, recording, History, columns | **Free, no ads** | `PR_FREE` 149; listing (§2.2) |
| Alternative message packs 「無駄機能」 (incl. 中二病 voice) | **Paid add-on, optional** | `MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` 54; e.g. "中二病コメントシステムを課金して買いました" *(I paid for the chūnibyō comment system)* (#858, `3667119040`, jp, 4★) |
| Add-on billing | **One-off ¥960–980 (2017–2021), monthly from 2022 per reviewers** | §9.G; `MON_SUBSCRIPTION` 7 |
| Donation link | **Unclear / intermittent** | "寄付" named in 23 reviews; #1425 (2020) says donations were not being accepted at the time |
| Trial | **None described** | no reviewer mentions a trial |

## 2.4 What could not be established

- The exact in-app purchase list and current prices (page not retrievable).
- When the add-on moved to monthly billing, beyond the first review describing it (2022-05).
- Whether the 2018 reset removal was later reversed in full. Reviews from 2020 on again describe a two-day reset prompt with a "don't reset" choice (#1545, #1832, #1878).

## 3.2 What users value, and why it works for them

### The design of small commitments (high-priority)

`PR_SIMPLE` **448** reviews, **19.56%** of 2,290 — High-priority signal, mean 4.90★, 2016-06-20 → 2026-09-06. `PR_CONCEPT` **262** reviews, **11.44%** of 2,290 — High-priority signal, mean 4.87★, 2016-08-27 → 2026-07-23. Reviewers explain the mechanism in their own words: a goal small enough to do on a bad day, one focus, a visible 30-day end, and a record that costs one tap.

> "最初は、「もう一個目標設定増やしてほしい！」などと言っていた私ですが、続けるうちに「これで良いのだ」と思うように。" — #1253, `5555608923`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: At first I was saying "let me add another goal!", but as I kept going I came to think "this is how it should be".)*

> "机に向かう『だけ』" — #2055, `11834726322`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: "just" sit at the desk)*

The small goal overflows into more: the #2055 reviewer studies about two hours a day. The same pattern appears in `OUT_RESULT` **157** reviews, **6.86%** of 2,290.

**Evidence:** `1439846457`, `2110654906`, `2572152821`, `3243544514`, `3629548265`, `4756954109`, `5942590562`, `7015679313`, `8612235128`, `11538765911`.

### Encouragement, reminders and humour (high-priority)

`PR_COMMENTS` **428** reviews, **18.69%** of 2,290 — High-priority signal, mean 4.93★, 2017-02-21 → 2026-09-06. `PR_NOTIF` **203** reviews, **8.86%** of 2,290 — High-priority signal, mean 4.84★, 2016-06-20 → 2026-09-03. `PR_HUMOR` **106** reviews, **4.63%** of 2,290. Many say they continue *in order to see tomorrow's line*.

> "「今日はまだやってません。やったれ！」" — #1999, `11117049969`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: "You haven't done it yet today. Go for it!")*

**Evidence:** `1397468700`, `1955114756`, `2550691625`, `3400808907`, `4348327131`, `5650531834`, `6913423332`, `8320654195`, `9609285212`, `11687767633`.

### Outcomes and use contexts

|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `OUT_STREAK` | Reports a streak / period completed | 617 | 26.94% | High-priority signal | 4.92 |
| `OUT_QUITTER` | Chronic quitter now continues ('三日坊主' overcome) | 299 | 13.06% | High-priority signal | 4.95 |

Streaks run long: 500 days (#2216), 1,000 days (#2247), a year or more (#1059, #1856, #2159, #2287). The goals are ordinary (sit-ups, one page of a book, one minute of tidying, brushing teeth), and reviewers report ordinary but real change:

> "無事に合格しました。" — #2089, `12259513979`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: I passed.)*

> "30日経った今でも服を溜めずに片付けてる自分偉いと褒めて。" — #1768, `8674571685`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: Even now, past 30 days, I put my clothes away instead of piling them up; praise me.)*

> "54歳のおじさんですが、20回の腕立て伏せを一カ月続けられました。" — #1882, `9679972581`, jp, 4★
> *(translation: I'm a 54-year-old man, and I kept up 20 push-ups a day for a month.)*

**Evidence:** `1783528882`, `2681120158`, `3605386774`, `5071407723`, `5901321372`, `6705947125`, `8463806345`, `9841863551`, `11252683284`, `12411436643`.

### Mental health and self-care context (meaningful)

`USE_WELLBEING` **25** reviews, **1.09%** of 2,290 — Meaningful signal, mean 4.92★, 2018-03-14 → 2026-08-08. Depression, ADHD, anxiety, autonomic disorders and binge eating are named as context, usually with a recovery story:

> "現在ではうつ病も緩和してきたので職場に復帰する事が出来ました。" — #1687, `8115938123`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: My depression has eased and I've been able to return to work.)*

> "30日毎朝歯磨きができたときは、『普通』に少し近づけたような気がして本当に嬉しかったです。" — #2072, `12055574930`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: When I managed to brush my teeth every morning for 30 days, I felt a little closer to "normal" and was really happy.)*

> "約8年悩まされたストレス性の深夜の過食症が治って15日目です。" — #2241, `14089761799`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: It's day 15 since the stress-driven late-night binge eating I'd suffered for about eight years stopped.)*

This is a sensitive audience. It matters for §3.6: a pushy tone that motivates one user can shame another.

**Evidence:** `2304355241`, `2586502097`, `3323521131`, `4119189488`, `5569223076`, `7028971398`, `8115938123`, `8968223399`, `12406404277`, `12853147159`.

### Free, no ads, no data (high-priority)

`PR_FREE` **149** reviews, **6.51%** of 2,290 — High-priority signal, mean 4.87★, 2017-08-29 → 2026-08-10. `PR_PRIVACY` **4** reviews, **0.17%** of 2,290 add that it collects no personal data. Its share rises from 1.4% of E1 to 11.2% of E3, a contrast reviewers draw with ad-supported and subscription-first apps they tried (`CMP_OTHER_APPS` 60).

> *(translation: This app doesn't smell of money at all.)*

## 3.3 Friction with the rules

`U_RULE_FRICTION` **114** reviews, **4.98%** of 2,290 — Very strong signal, mean 4.06★, 2016-06-24 → 2026-09-06. Mean rating is below the corpus mean; most of these reviewers still rate 4–5★ and ask for flexibility, not removal.

| Code | Meaning | n | % of 2,290 | Band | Mean ★ |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `REQ_MULTI` | Wants more than one habit | 44 | 1.92% | Meaningful signal | 4.39 |
| `NEG_RESET_RULE` | Dislikes the 2-day reset | 14 | 0.61% | Emerging signal | 3.21 |
| `REQ_RESET_BACK` | Wants the automatic reset back (or optional) | 13 | 0.57% | Emerging signal | 4.08 |
| `REQ_PERIOD` | Wants shorter / custom periods | 13 | 0.57% | Emerging signal | 4.69 |
| `NEG_DAYBOUNDARY` | Day rollover time is a problem | 11 | 0.48% | Weak signal | 3.45 |
| `NEG_PREVDAY_FLOW` | Previous-day entry / backfill is awkward | 11 | 0.48% | Weak signal | 3.45 |
| `NEG_RULES_UNCLEAR` | Rules not explained in the app | 9 | 0.39% | Weak signal | 4.11 |
| `REQ_DAYBOUNDARY_SET` | Wants to set the day-change / reset time | 6 | 0.26% | Weak signal | 3.83 |
| `NEG_PERIOD_FIXED` | Fixed challenge lengths | 5 | 0.22% | Weak signal | 3.40 |
| `NEG_90_REMOVED` | Objects to losing the 90-day option | 3 | 0.13% | Weak signal | 4.67 |
| `REQ_PAUSE` | Wants to pause / skip days (illness, period, travel) | 10 | 0.44% | Weak signal | 4.00 |
| `REQ_FREQ` | Wants non-daily frequency | 8 | 0.35% | Weak signal | 4.38 |

- **Day boundary.** Night workers and late studiers get caught between midnight and 3 a.m.: "前日に15:00頃入力すると次の日に10:00頃にやっても入力が出来ません。" *(if I record at 15:00 one day, I can't record at 10:00 the next)* (#1386, `6059617063`, jp, 3★). A night-shift worker values the opposite side of the same rule: "日付を跨いだ時には、前日か本日か選択できるのも夜勤者にとっては嬉しい機能です。" *(choosing yesterday or today after midnight is welcome for night workers)* (#2284, `14505391902`, jp, 3★).
- **Longer cycles.** People who finish 30 days want to keep counting rather than restart the same goal: "30日後もどれだけ記録を延ばせるかチャレンジしたい！" *(I want to see how far I can extend the record beyond 30 days!)* (#2156, `12910203779`, jp, 5★).
- **No visible dates.** "日付が表示されないため､3日目あたりから記録を付けたのに！とか付けてないはずなのに！となって､よくわからなくなってしまってアンストしました。" *(because no dates are shown, from about day 3 I lost track of whether I'd recorded or not, got confused and uninstalled)* (#1774, `8693987085`, jp, 3★).

**Evidence:** `1399663066`, `1635956586`, `2231712854`, `2634793204`, `3620821414`, `5382343965`, `6602410611`, `8558871776`, `11021909746`, `12884112714`.

## 3.4 Reliability

`U_BUG_ANY` **104** reviews, **4.54%** of 2,290 — Very strong signal, mean 3.29★, 2017-02-27 → 2026-08-14. Defect share falls from 7.6% of E1 to 1.9% of E5, but the recording defects persist across a decade.

| Code | Meaning | n | % of 2,290 | Band | Mean ★ |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `BUG_NOTIF` | Wrong / unwanted / missing notifications | 38 | 1.66% | Meaningful signal | 3.42 |
| `BUG_INPUT` | Cannot record a day | 20 | 0.87% | Emerging signal | 2.75 |
| `BUG_COUNT` | Day count wrong | 12 | 0.52% | Emerging signal | 2.83 |
| `BUG_DISPLAY` | Wrong label / display glitch | 13 | 0.57% | Emerging signal | 4.15 |
| `BUG_RESET` | Unexpected reset | 7 | 0.31% | Weak signal | 3.43 |
| `BUG_CRASH` | Crashes | 5 | 0.22% | Weak signal | 1.80 |
| `BUG_IAP` | In-app purchase stuck / not restored | 4 | 0.17% | Weak signal | 3.25 |
| `BUG_KEYBOARD` | Keyboard hides button in setup | 3 | 0.13% | Weak signal | 3.33 |
| `BUG_LANG` | Language switched unexpectedly after update | 2 | 0.09% | Ignore by default | 5.00 |
| `BUG_GENERIC` | Stopped working (unspecified bug) | 2 | 0.09% | Ignore by default | 3.00 |
| `BUG_BATTERY` | Battery drain | 1 | 0.04% | Ignore by default | 1.00 |

- **Notifications.** Missing, duplicated after a new cycle, continuing for a deleted goal, or arriving after being switched off (#603, #881, #1946, #1954).
- **Recording blocked.** Tapping the ring shows the undo hint instead of recording, often from day 2 or 3 (#883, #1034, #1314, #1531, #2014).
- **Wrong counts and phantom resets.** "Unrecorded" flags on unscheduled weekdays, counts stuck at 7 or 4, the previous day erased (#772, #1340, #1450, #1875, #2218, #2227).
- A reviewer who uses several of the developer's apps generalises: "どれも何ヶ月かすると、ありえないほどバグの連続が発生します。" *(after a few months, all of them start producing an absurd run of bugs)* (#1629, `7566282416`, jp, 5★).

**Evidence:** `1553783592`, `1960898756`, `2104964546`, `2577618701`, `3719986876`, `4677332909`, `5998212733`, `6614785939`, `7566282416`, `10172688755`.

## 3.6 Tone and notification backlash

14 reviews (0.61% of 2,290, emerging signal), mean 2.50★, 2018-03-09 → 2025-06-20. `NEG_COMMENT_TONE` **7** reviews, **0.31%** of 2,290; `NEG_NOTIF_ANNOY` **9** reviews, **0.39%** of 2,290. Before 2023 this complaint appears 3 times; from 2023 11 times.

> "応援のテンションとノリに鳥肌が立つ" — #1938, `10317633924`, jp, 1★
> *(translation: The cheerleading energy and jokiness give me goosebumps.)*

> "「本っっっっ当に必要な時だけ」とふざけた念押しをしてくる" — #1921, `10058846156`, jp, 3★
> *(translation: it gives a flippant "only when you reeeeally need to" warning)*

> "日中ずっとバッジで責められている気がして気が重くなってしまう" — #2149, `12796168055`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: the badge makes me feel blamed all day and weighs on me)*

**All IDs:** `2285682176`, `3959571379`, `7553106036`, `10058846156`, `10271794451`, `10317633924`, `10475786716`, `10527702382`, `10548585183`, `10598709745`, `10830806624`, `11568521715`, `12446202458`, `12796168055`.

## 3.7 Failure, churn and "not for me"

`FAIL_ADMIT` **37** reviews, **1.62%** of 2,290 — Meaningful signal, mean 4.14★, 2016-10-01 → 2026-09-04: reviewers who say they could not keep it up, usually blaming themselves. `U_CHURN` **20** reviews, **0.87%** of 2,290 — Emerging signal, mean 1.90★, 2017-06-30 → 2026-06-19. `NEG_NOT_FOR_ME` **9** reviews, **0.39%** of 2,290.

The critical point is that recording is itself a habit the app asks for, and for some users it is the one that fails:

> "わたしは「このアプリを毎日継続して開くこと」すらできませんでした。" — #1656, `7788169534`, jp, 1★
> *(translation: I couldn't even keep up "opening this app every day".)*

> "アプリ開いて記録するのが続かなくなった" — #2202, `13609275319`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: I stopped keeping up with opening the app to record)*

> "これもうゲームのログインボーナスと変わらないよ。" — #2098, `12319745941`, jp, 2★
> *(translation: This is no different from a game's daily login bonus.)*

#1656 has 12 votes and #2202 is titled "reading now sticks, but…": the habit survived and the app did not. Reviewers asking for a widget (`REQ_WIDGET` **17** reviews, **0.74%** of 2,290) are asking for the same thing, a way to keep the goal visible without opening the app. #2078 describes a widget in another app doing exactly that.

**Evidence:** `1459109041`, `1965350242`, `2932229708`, `4681844471`, `6073433446`, `6761375234`, `7391018322`, `8558871776`, `10236767646`, `11071967308`, `12408528616`, `13962093958`.

## 4.2 Themes by star band

### 5★ — 1,948 reviews (85.07%)

**Why 5★.** Simplicity, the daily encouragement line and the reviewer's own streak, usually all three, often written on a milestone day. The developer's attitude and the absence of ads and forced payment are the next reasons. 5★ reviews also carry feature requests (`REQ_MULTI`, `REQ_WIDGET`), so a request is not a complaint here.

## 4.3 Rating-versus-text contradictions

`CONTRA_RATING` **17** reviews, **0.74%** of 2,290. 17 of 17 are 1–3★ reviews whose text is wholly positive, e.g. "使いやすい、続けやすい、👍🏼" *(easy to use, easy to keep going)* (#437, `2537509206`, jp, 1★) under 1★. `CONTRA_TEXT` **1** reviews, **0.04%** of 2,290: a title claiming 15 days over a body saying it was installed today (#1506). All are kept and coded by their text.

**All contradiction IDs:** `1800248685`, `1897152268`, `2211291320`, `2537509206`, `2593461483`, `2607290645`, `2622230053`, `2663111072`, `3124919223`, `3295545716`, `3330643231`, `4309212365`, `4601676849`, `4946402831`, `6768037999`, `13216911697`, `14121939810`, `14505391902`.

# PART 5 — PAID-USER AND UPGRADE ANALYSIS

## 5.1 The populations, and what they are not

- **Deliberate purchasers: `MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` 54 (2.36% of 2,290)**, mean 4.76★: say they bought the add-on.
- **Stated intent: `MON_WILL_PAY` 90 (3.93%)**, mean 4.89★: say they will or would buy ("after 30 days", "when I have money").
- **Stated barrier: 39 reviews (1.70%)**: price too high, can't pay, or objecting to monthly billing.

**None of these is a conversion rate.** The corpus says nothing about how many users pay.

## 5.2 What triggers a purchase

Within the 54 purchasers (segment rates):

1. **Support for the developer**: `MON_SUPPORT_DEV` 14 of 54 purchasers (25.93%); with any developer-bond code 19 of 54 (35.19%).
2. **A completed milestone, used as a reward**: `OUT_STREAK` 22 of 54 purchasers (40.74%). Intent is often conditional on it: "30日続けることができたので無駄機能買いました" *(I kept it up for 30 days, so I bought the useless feature)* (#1476, `6581562527`, jp, 5★); "一ヶ月続いたら無駄機能買います" *(I'll buy the useless feature if I last a month)* (#1172, `5246053794`, jp, 5★).
3. **More or different encouragement**: `PR_COMMENTS` 14 of 54 purchasers (25.93%). The free lines repeat every 30-day cycle, and reviewers notice (#1798, `8886936066`, jp, 5★).
4. **Paying as a commitment device**: `MON_PAY_COMMIT` **3** reviews, **0.13%** of 2,290, e.g. "課金することでさらに義務感を生み出せる" *(paying creates more sense of obligation)* (#820, `3598813740`, jp, 5★).

## 5.3 Segment rates among purchasers

Denominator: **54** purchasers. Global % shown for comparison. Segment rates, not corpus bands.

| Theme | n in segment | % of segment | % of all 2,290 | Lift |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `MON_SUPPORT_DEV` | 14 | 25.9% | 1.7% | 15.22× |
| `U_DEV_BOND` | 19 | 35.2% | 15.9% | 2.21× |
| `OUT_STREAK` | 22 | 40.7% | 26.9% | 1.51× |
| `PR_COMMENTS` | 14 | 25.9% | 18.7% | 1.39× |
| `PR_TIPS` | 7 | 13.0% | 4.5% | 2.91× |
| `PR_FREE` | 7 | 13.0% | 6.5% | 1.99× |
| `OUT_RESULT` | 7 | 13.0% | 6.9% | 1.89× |
| `USE_FITNESS` | 7 | 13.0% | 8.4% | 1.55× |
| `REV_MILESTONE` | 3 | 5.6% | 0.5% | 10.60× |
| `CHURN_RETURN` | 3 | 5.6% | 0.9% | 6.06× |
| `U_BUG_ANY` | 3 | 5.6% | 4.5% | 1.22× |
| `MON_PAID_DISAPPOINT` | 2 | 3.7% | 0.1% | 42.41× |
| `SUP_NONE` | 2 | 3.7% | 0.2% | 16.96× |

## 5.4 What purchasers report

Most purchasers are satisfied (mean 4.76★). The add-on is valued for variety and fun, in particular the 中二病 voice:

> "課金したらコメントがちょっぴりグレードアップするのもとても好きです。課金して正解でした！" — #1333, `5906598071`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: I love that paying upgrades the comments a little. Paying was the right call!)*

The purchasers who were unhappy name specific failures (4 of 54): content that felt no different (#1577), quotations taken from famous people rather than original lines (#1057), a purchase that kept being advertised after buying (#1577), recording that broke after buying with no reply from support (#1409, `6173882157`, jp, 1★).

## 5.5 Upgrade barriers among non-buyers

| Code | Meaning | n | % of 2,290 | Band | Mean ★ |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `MON_PRICE_HIGH` | IAP price too high | 20 | 0.87% | Emerging signal | 4.65 |
| `MON_NO_PAY` | Cannot or will not pay | 17 | 0.74% | Emerging signal | 5.00 |
| `MON_SUBSCRIPTION` | Add-on is (now) a monthly subscription / wants annual plan | 7 | 0.31% | Weak signal | 4.71 |
| `NEG_UPSELL_NAG` | Repeated purchase prompts | 5 | 0.22% | Weak signal | 1.40 |
| `MON_ACCIDENTAL` | Accidental / unwanted purchase | 2 | 0.09% | Ignore by default | 1.00 |

- **Price level.** Named thresholds: ¥100–300 (#479, #1143, #1460), ¥500 (#1179), ¥100 repeatable donations (#1707, #2177).
- **Monthly billing.** Reviewers who would support once resist a recurring charge: "一回限りよ課金もあるといいです。" *(a one-time purchase option would be nice too)* (#1922, `10068902732`, jp, 5★); "かつては買い切りだったようだが月額に変わってしまってるのも残念" *(it seems it used to be a one-off purchase and has changed to monthly, which is a shame)* (#1798, `8886936066`, jp, 5★).
- **Students and minors.** "自分の家、課金ダメって言われてる" *(my family says no in-app purchases)* (#1230, `5441799315`, jp, 5★).
- **Non-monetary support offered instead.** "メニュー欄に「広告を見る」ボタンを追加してはいかがでしょうか！" *(how about adding a "watch an ad" button to the menu?)* (#2133, `12677032119`, jp, 5★); two reviewers say they bought the developer's book as support (#2142, #2272).

## 5.6 Refund and churn drivers after purchase

Only 2 reviews ask for money back, both about a purchase they did not mean to make or did not receive: "無駄機能のオフの仕方が分からず、、" *(I couldn't work out how to turn the useless feature off)* (#806, `3554026149`, jp, 1★) (then charged ¥960); #1473, `6572812402`, jp, 1★ (paid, feature not delivered, no reply). Unwanted purchase prompts produce the harshest words in the corpus: "しかも誤課金しやすい画面。970円も誤課金した。" *(and the screen makes mis-purchases easy. I mistakenly paid ¥970)* (#661, `3209298921`, jp, 1★); "いきなりの金銭要求。" *(a sudden demand for money)* (#2129, `12643702172`, jp, 1★).

## 5.7 What cannot be claimed here

- The share of users who pay, renew or cancel the monthly add-on.
- Whether the move to monthly billing changed revenue or goodwill. Price complaints stay under 2% of every era (§7.2).
- The current price, beyond reviewer statements.

Japan *is* the corpus, so every finding in Parts 0–5 is a Japan finding. Features specific to the Japanese market are the vocabulary and the voice: the 三日坊主 ("three-day monk", quitter) identity appears in 343 reviews, and the 中二病 message pack is a local cultural joke that purchasers cite. Japan-specific rule friction is the late-night day boundary for students and shift workers (§3.3). The JP public rating is 4.78★ from 53,106 ratings.

- **High-review-volume group** = storefronts with ≥50 reviews in this corpus: jp.

# PART 7 — TIME-TREND ANALYSIS

## 7.1 Method

Eras are cut at points where reviewers describe a product change, fixed before the era tables were computed. The timeline checks in §9.C assert the boundaries that matter.

- **E1 → E2 at 2018-03-14**: the first reviews welcoming the removal of the automatic reset and objecting to the loss of the 90-day option.
- **E2 → E3 at 2020-01-01**: a calendar cut, with no product event, to split the 2018–2019 volume peak from the long tail.
- **E3 → E4 at 2022-05-01**: the first review describing the add-on as a monthly charge.
- **E4 → E5 at 2025-01-01**: a calendar cut for the recent period.

| Era | Name | Dates | Reviews | Mean ★ | 1–2★ % | 5★ % |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| E1 | Launch rules (reset + 90-day option) | 2016-06-20 → 2018-03-12 | 356 | 4.65 | 4.8% | 80.9% |
| E2 | Reset removed, peak volume | 2018-03-14 → 2019-12-31 | 844 | 4.76 | 2.8% | 85.5% |
| E3 | 2020 → spring 2022 | 2020-01-01 → 2022-04-29 | 563 | 4.77 | 3.0% | 87.2% |
| E4 | Add-on billed monthly | 2022-05-01 → 2024-12-30 | 316 | 4.72 | 3.8% | 85.1% |
| E5 | Recent | 2025-01-02 → 2026-09-06 | 211 | 4.71 | 2.8% | 84.4% |

## 7.3 Yearly series

| Year | Reviews | Mean ★ | 1–2★ | Defect | Rule friction | Requests | Paid signal | Tone/notif. |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|

## 7.4 What changed

**E1 (launch, 356 reviews, 4.65★).** Early crashes on setup (all four `BUG_CRASH` 1★ are 2017) and awkward backfill of yesterday (`NEG_PREVDAY_FLOW` 7 of 11). Defects at 7.6% of E1, the highest of any era. Praise of simplicity at its peak (25.6%).

**E2 (reset removed, peak volume, 844 reviews, 4.76★).** The March 2018 update splits users on the reset (§0.4). Volume peaks in 2018-05 and 2018-06 (§9.H). The developer relationship is strongest: `PR_DEV` 18.5% of E2. From 2018-06 reviewers start installing the sister app 「集中」 ("Focus").

**E3 (2020 → spring 2022, 563 reviews, 4.77★).** Pandemic-era reviews (#1286, #1331, #1335 mention closures). Praise of being free and ad-free peaks (11.2%), and so does cross-use of the developer's other apps (6.7%). Paid signal is highest here (10.5%).

**E4 (add-on billed monthly, 316 reviews, 4.72★).** Monthly billing appears. Explicit purchases fall to 1.6% of E4, from 3.7% in E3, while intent stays mentioned. This is directional, and review text cannot show causation. Tone and notification backlash concentrates here (9 of 14). Fitness and diet goals become the most common use (14.2% fitness), and reported concrete results rise (12.0%).

**E5 (recent, 211 reviews, 4.71★; directional).** Requests reach 14.2% of reviews, led by multiple goals (4.7%) and the widget (2.4%). Developer praise is at its lowest (5.2%). The developer's book appears alongside the app (#2072, #2216, #2253), and new acquisition routes appear: a YouTube appearance (#2262) and an AI assistant's recommendation:

> "ＡＩにこういったアプリのオススメを聞き、その中から選びました。" — #2257, `14219888171`, jp, 5★
> *(translation: I asked an AI to recommend apps like this and picked from those.)*

## 7.5 What persisted

- **The core praise.** Praise codes are present in 70.9–79.4% of every era; streak outcomes in every era.
- **Recording and notification defects**, from 2017-12-15 to 2026-08-14.
- **The reset argument**, from 2016-10-18 to 2026-09-04.
- **Price sensitivity to the add-on**, in every era from 2017 (`MON_PRICE_HIGH` 20, 2017-11-20 → 2025-09-19).

## 7.6 Sensitivity — does the 2018 peak drive the conclusions?

| Headline share | All 2,290 | Excluding E2 | E4 + E5 only |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `U_PRAISE_ANY` | 75.7% | 73.5% | 71.5% |
| `U_OUTCOME` | 43.3% | 45.4% | 48.4% |
| `PR_COMMENTS` | 18.7% | 20.5% | 20.9% |
| `U_DEV_BOND` | 15.9% | 13.3% | 8.9% |
| `U_BUG_ANY` | 4.5% | 5.1% | 2.7% |
| `U_RULE_FRICTION` | 5.0% | 5.5% | 6.5% |
| `U_REQ_ANY` | 9.3% | 10.2% | 14.6% |
| `REQ_MULTI` | 1.9% | 1.9% | 2.8% |
| `U_PAID` | 6.7% | 7.1% | 4.9% |

Praise and outcomes are stable under every cut. The requests and the multiple-goal ask are *larger* in the recent period, and the developer bond smaller.

# PART 8 — PRODUCT IMPLICATIONS

Each item cites the findings it rests on. **Immediate fixes** address defects and harms already reported; **experiments** test a hypothesis; **research questions** need data the corpus cannot give.

## 8.1 Immediate fix — never lose a recorded day (§0.5, §3.4)

- Eliminate the undo-hint loop that blocks recording (`BUG_INPUT` 20), phantom resets and erased days (`BUG_RESET` 7, `BUG_COUNT` 12). Log every tap server-side or on device so a lost day can be restored.
- Show the date being recorded on the ring (requested in #1774, #1798, #2029, #2285). Most day-boundary confusion comes from not seeing which day a tap counts for.

## 8.2 Immediate fix — notifications that obey (§0.2, §3.4, §3.6)

- Honour notifications-off, remove notifications for deleted or completed goals, prevent duplicates on a new cycle (`BUG_NOTIF` 38; #1946, #1954).
- Allow weekday/weekend reminder times and later edits (`REQ_NOTIF_EDIT` 10).

## 8.3 Immediate fix — a quiet mode for the voice (§0.2, §3.6)

- One setting that switches the encouragement line, the "are you sure" jokes and the badge to plain, neutral text. It rests on 14 tone/notification complaints, mean 2.50★, concentrated since 2023, and on the sensitive audience in §3.2 (`USE_WELLBEING` 25). Keep the voice as the default; the praise for it is 428 reviews.

## 8.4 Immediate fix — no purchase screen that can be tapped by accident (§5.5, §5.6)

- Require an explicit confirmation on the add-on, stop showing it to people who already bought, and add a clear way to turn a pack off (`MON_ACCIDENTAL` 2, `NEG_UPSELL_NAG` 5, `BUG_IAP` 4; #661, #806, #1577, #2129). These are few (mean 1.33★ for accidental purchases and nagging prompts), and #2129 calls the app 「詐欺に近い」 (close to fraud).

## 8.5 Experiment — let a finished habit stay (§0.3, §3.5)

- After a 30-day cycle, offer "keep tracking this quietly" alongside "set the next goal": one active goal with reminders and encouragement, and graduated habits kept as a low-key streak. This answers most of `REQ_MULTI` (44) and `REQ_PERIOD` (13) without giving up the one-goal discipline that 148 reviewers credit. Test against 60- and 90-day continuation rates, not number of goals created.

## 8.6 Experiment — a pause that is honest (§0.4, §3.3)

- A limited "rest day" (e.g. two per cycle) for illness, periods and travel, plus an optional strict mode that resets after one missed day for those who want it (`REQ_PAUSE` 10, `REQ_RESET_BACK` 13, `NEG_RESET_RULE` 14; #2024 asks for the strict version). Both camps have existed since 2018, so a single rule will keep losing one of them.

## 8.7 Experiment — make support cheap to give (§0.6, §5.5)

- Add a small repeatable tip (¥100–300) or a one-time pack alongside the monthly plan. Reviewers name these amounts (20 price objections; #1262, #1707, #2177) and describe paying out of gratitude (39). Offer the prompt at the moment reviewers already choose to pay: completing a 30-day cycle (§5.2).
- Refresh the free message set between cycles, or say plainly that a second cycle repeats it. The repetition is the add-on's main selling point and also a complaint (`REQ_MORE_COMMENTS` 21).

## 8.8 Experiment — keep the goal visible without opening the app (§3.7)

- Home-screen and lock-screen widgets showing today's status (`REQ_WIDGET` 17; #2078, #2127), and recording from the notification (`REQ_NOTIF_ACTION` 1). Hypothesis: users for whom the habit sticks but the app doesn't (#1656, #2202) are lost at the "open app" step.

## 8.9 What the corpus says to keep

- The guided setup that shrinks goals ("can you really do that in 5 minutes?"), one goal, one tap (§0.1).
- The encouragement line and the humour, as the default (§3.2).
- No ads, no data collection, and no forced payment. These are named as reasons to trust and to pay (§3.2, §5.2).
- The developer's voice, in columns and release notes. It is what purchasers say they are paying for (§0.7).

## 8.10 Research questions the corpus cannot answer

1. What share of new users complete a first 30-day cycle, and where do the rest stop: setup, day 2–3 recording, or after the first reset?
2. How did explicit purchases and cancellations change when the add-on moved to monthly billing?
3. Do users who finish a cycle and do not set a new goal keep the habit? (§3.7 suggests some do.)
4. How often does the recording defect in §3.4 occur per active user, and on which OS versions and devices?
5. How do the cn and tw audiences retain compared with Japan?

# PART 9 — EVIDENCE APPENDIX

## 9.A Counting rules

1. **Denominator.** Global = 2,290. Star band, era, storefront and purchaser denominators are stated at each use.
2. **Non-exclusive.** A review is counted once in each theme it carries. Percentages sum past 100%.
3. **Codes come from the text, never from the star rating.** A 1★ whose text is praise is coded as praise and flagged `CONTRA_RATING`.
4. **Requests vs defects.** "It doesn't have X" is `REQ_*`; "X doesn't work" is `BUG_*`; "the rule is too strict" is `NEG_*` or `REQ_*` under rule friction; a price objection is `MON_*`.
5. **`MON_PAID_EXPLICIT` requires a statement of purchase.** Intent ("I will buy") is `MON_WILL_PAY`. Buying the developer's book is `MKT_BOOK`, not an app purchase.
6. **`OUT_*` outcomes are first-person.** Anticipation ("I think I can keep going") is `JUST_STARTED`.
7. **Unions are derived, never hand-applied**; mechanical codes (`STAR_n`, `ERA_En`) come from the record.
8. **Signal bands** are computed from count ÷ denominator using §1.5.

## 9.C Validation — 628 checks, 0 failures

`70-validate.py` output, verbatim:

```
628 checks, 0 failures
  coverage: 8
  codes: 4
  theme_map: 417
  unions: 18
  mech: 6
  timeline: 5
  semantic: 6
  lang: 6
  bounds: 158
```

The timeline checks were written before Part 7 and would fail if its era narrative were back-fitted: no reset-removal praise or reset-return request before 2018-03-14; the 90-day complaint only in 2018; no monthly-billing complaint before 2022; no sister-app mention before 2018-06. The semantic checks require every 1–2★ review to carry a negative, request, failure or noise code (or `CONTRA_RATING`).

### What earlier runs caught

1. **Drifted review IDs.** In the first nine batches, 115 lines carried a neighbouring review's ID while their codes and notes described the correct review. Found when the alignment check began verifying quoted fragments; repaired from the index only where every quote verified, and two further lines by hand.
2. **Paraphrased quotations.** 13 notes quoted loosely; rewritten to the exact text. One code (`REV_FIRST` on #35) had been taken from the author's nickname rather than the text and was removed.
3. **#1486** carried `MON_SUPPORT_DEV` for "makes me want to cheer the developer", with no money involved; removed. The support check was widened to accept a book purchase (#2272).
4. **The kana check was wrong, not the data.** #1178 is written in half-width katakana; the script range was extended.

### What validation cannot check

Whether a code is the *right* judgement. That is auditable only by reading the note on each line of `70-cls/`, which quotes the evidence.

## 9.D Sensitivity

See §7.6. One further stress test removes the reviews most likely to be enthusiasm on day one:

| Removed | Reviews removed | `U_OUTCOME` share of remaining | `U_PRAISE_ANY` share of remaining |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `JUST_STARTED` (written in the first days) | 89 | 987 of 2,201 (44.84%) | 1668 of 2,201 (75.78%) |
| `REV_NOT_USED` + `JUST_STARTED` | 93 | 987 of 2,197 (44.92%) | 1664 of 2,197 (75.74%) |
| All 1-to-10-character bodies | 146 | 966 of 2,144 (45.06%) | 1624 of 2,144 (75.75%) |

## 9.H Burst, solicitation and authenticity checks

| Check | Result | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| Repeat author names | 0 | none |
| Duplicate bodies (≥5 chars) across accounts | 8 groups: #76 #133 #580; #149 #1013 #1138 #1246; #182 #395 #780; #200 #437; #227 #977 #1426; #819 #2070; #1172 #1616; #1324 #2168 | short generic phrases ("シンプルで使いやすい", "良い"), months to years apart; not a coordinated pattern |
| Busiest months | 2018-05 (100), 2018-06 (95), 2018-10 (55) | 2018-05/06 peak: 195 reviews, mean 4.74★, outcome share 42.1% vs 43.3% overall. Content matches the rest of the corpus; cause not visible in the data |
| Busiest days | 2018-05-27 (13), 2018-06-11 (9), 2018-05-14 (8) | |
| Reviews with votes | 227 (9.91%) | max 354 (#1349) |
| Edited reviews | 11 | #2285 is edited to thank the developer for a reply; follow-ups also appear inside unedited bodies (#302, #1349) |
| In-app "rate this app" link mentioned | 5 | a solicitation channel exists; several report it failing to open the store (#869, #1073, #1517) |
| Reviewer pre-empts a stealth-marketing suspicion (`SUS_DISCLAIM`) | 9 | e.g. "やらせかと思うコメントばかりで躊躇いましたか？私は躊躇いました。" *(did all these comments look staged and make you hesitate? they made me hesitate)* (#1435, `6322195505`, jp, 5★) |
| Reviewer accuses reviews of being fake (`SUS_ACCUSE`) | 1 | "こんなバグがあって評価高いのはサクラを疑います。" *(with bugs like this, such high ratings make me suspect fake reviews)* (#1689, `8136661902`, jp, 1★) |
| `CONTRA_RATING` | 17 | §4.3 |

**Reading:** no evidence of seeded or duplicated positive reviews was found. The corpus's positivity is consistent with the public rating from 53,106 ratings, the milestone-review habit and an in-app review link. Selection toward satisfied users is the bias to keep in mind, not fabrication.

## 9.I Developer responsiveness — the evidence both ways

Developer replies are not in the dataset. Reviewers describe them: `DEV_REPLY_SEEN` **14** reviews, **0.61%** of 2,290 mention replies to reviews, including one who installed the app because of them (#184) and one who edited a review to thank the developer for replying (#2285). A reviewer thanks an update for fixing their problem (#75, `SUP_FIXED` 1), and another is delighted to be answered in the release notes (#78). On the other side, `SUP_NONE` **5** reviews, **0.22%** of 2,290 report emails with no answer, about a purchase (#1409, #1473, #1577), a notification defect (#1654) or a feature request (#1539).
